There’ve been a few weeks where I felt more normal, like I was getting stuff done, but I’d still hear that supervisor voice, or feel like the old ticketing system was flagging me somehow.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when the voices lasted for at least 2 weeks while you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. There were a few weeks where my mood was more normal, like I was actually getting things done, but I still heard that supervisor voice… or felt like the old ticketing software was flagging me.\n\nDoctor: Have you taken any antipsychotic medicines recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure… I don’t think so recently, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a tremor in your hands when they’re resting?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I haven’t really noticed my hands shaking when I’m just sitting still.\n\nDoctor: Has there been a period of at least 2 weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there’s been stretches like that. It’s been on and off, but mostly there for… about two years, I guess. A little over two years. It eases up sometimes, but not for long.\n\nDoctor: During those depressed stretches, did you ever feel hopeless nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe sometimes. I wouldn’t say I thought about it that clearly every day. It was more just… feeling low and stuck, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices or the feeling that the old ticketing system was flagging you been going on for at least 6 months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s been longer than six months. Maybe around a year and a half, closer to two. And it wasn’t only when I was really depressed.\n\nDoctor: During those low periods, do you lose interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. Stuff like sketching, or taking notes on coffee I try, even weekend hikes… I still start to do them sometimes, but it’s like there’s nothing there. I open the notebook or put my boots on and just… don’t feel drawn to it.\n\nDoctor: When someone else moves your arm or body into a position, does it tend to stay there for a while?\n\nPatient: Not really like someone moves me and I stay that way. It’s more… when I’m overwhelmed, I’ll notice I’m just standing there with my mug halfway up, or pacing the same little loop by the couch. I don’t always realize right away.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you stopped speaking altogether, even though you were awake?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I might get quiet, like… not want to answer, but I don’t remember being awake and just completely unable to talk.\n\nDoctor: Before the voices or the feeling that the ticketing system was flagging you ever started, had you already gone through a period of at least two weeks of feeling depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. The low part was there before that, for a long time. Like, not just a week or two—more like couple years, with only short stretches where it let up a bit.\n\nDoctor: During those low periods, did you feel worthless nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I’d get stuck replaying little mistakes from work, like a slow reply or something from a contract job, and it would feel like I’d let everyone down. Like maybe I deserved getting fired.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over the whole time you’ve been ill, have the voices been present during most of that time, including times when you were not depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. There were a few stretches, like a couple weeks maybe, where my mood was more normal and I was getting stuff done, but I’d still hear that supervisor voice sometimes, or feel like the old ticketing system was flagging me.\n\nDoctor: Since these problems started, has your ability to work or take care of yourself gotten a lot worse?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has gotten worse. I mean, I’m still managing to keep a place and get by, but work’s been harder. I miss deadlines, avoid calls sometimes, and then I guess I come off unreliable.\n\nAnd outside of work… I don’t see people as much. Basic stuff like meals, errands, even hiking, I kind of let slide unless I really push myself.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over the whole time you’ve been ill, have you been depressed for most of that time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think most of the time, honestly. It’s not always as bad every week, but the sadness and guilt are kind of… there in the background.
